# Nursery 2 — Third Term

*Pupil's book · every subject offered to this class, every teaching week of the term.*

**11 subjects · 105 lessons · 13 sections.** Weeks 7, 10, 11 and 12 are Mid-Term Break, Revision, Examination and Closing: no lesson is written for a week the school keeps for those, except where the school's own table prints a teaching row there.

Each lesson ends with a worksheet and a piece of my own work; each subject ends with the term practice paper — 30 objective, 10 sub-objective and 5 theory questions. The contents page lists every part and every lesson of this book.

# Basic Science

Weeks 1 to 6, 8 and 9. Week 7 is Mid-Term Break, Week 10 Revision, Week 11 Examination,
Week 12 Closing — those weeks carry no note.
*Depth: NERDC 2025 · Nursery 3 · Basic Science and Technology · Third Term, pp 497–502; Week 9
exploring the environment is taken from Primary 1 Basic Science, First Term W1–2, pp 624–625.*


### WEEK 1 — WATER: HOW WE KEEP IT CLEAN AND SAFE

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Science and Technology · Third Term W2 (pp 497–502).*

**You will learn to**

• say why we keep water in a covered container
• name the containers we store water in at home
• tell how to keep stored water clean
• wash a container before I use it

**Things to know**

• **Water keeps best** in a container that is **clean** and has a **lid**.
• A lid keeps out **dirt**, **dust**, **flies** and **small insects** that want to fall in.
• Containers we use for water: a **covered bucket**, a **kettle with a lid**, a **water tank**, a
  **jerry can**, a **clay pot (agolo)**, a **bottle**.
• A container used for water should **never** be used for paraffin, insecticide or soap.
• Before water goes in, the container is **washed out with clean water** first.
• We **dip a cup or a ladle** into the water; we do not put our hands, our legs or a dirty sponge in it.
• Stored water can still become **dirty**: an open bucket collects dust, and a bucket on the floor
  collects dirt from the feet of everybody who passes.
• Keep the container on a **stand** or a **bench**, covered, away from the cooking place and the toilet.
• Once a week the container is **emptied, washed and rinsed**, then filled again.
• **Rain water** is caught in a clean tank or drum with a cover; **borehole, well and tap water** come
  from the ground or the pipe.

**Words for my notebook**

We store water in a clean covered container so that dirt and insects cannot get in. The container is
washed before use and kept on a stand, away from the floor and the cooking place.

**Let us talk**

1. Which container keeps water cleaner — the open bucket or the bucket with a lid?
2. What falls into water that is left uncovered?
3. Show me how you carry water home without spilling it.
4. Why is a jerry can a good container for water?


![A clay pot with a cup hanging at its neck, a jerry can with its lid screwed on, and a bucket on a wooden stand while a girl lifts the lid.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-science/week1-covered-containers.png)
**Worksheet**

1. A good water container must always be A) painted B) clean and covered C) sweet D) left on the floor
2. Which of these is used to store water? A) insecticide drum B) open plate C) covered bucket D) sieve
3. Before we put water in a container we should A) wash it out B) burn it C) fill it with sand D) leave it open
4. Water stored in an open bucket becomes dirty because A) it is cold B) it is heavy C) dust and insects fall in D) the bucket is blue
5. Fill in: We keep the water container on a ____ or a bench, not on the floor.
6. Mention two containers you have seen used for water at home.
7. Write two sentences on how your family keeps its water clean.

**My own work**

Draw the water container in your house with its lid on, then colour the lid the colour of the real one.

### WEEK 2 — WATER-BORNE DISEASES: SICKNESS THAT COMES WITH DIRTY WATER

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Science and Technology · Third Term W3 (pp 497–502).*

**You will learn to**

• say what a water-borne disease is
• name diseases that come from dirty water
• tell how dirty water makes us sick
• say how to stop these sicknesses

**Things to know**

• A **water-borne disease** is a sickness that comes to us from **dirty water** or from water with
  germs in it.
• The ones we must know: **diarrhoea**, **cholera**, **typhoid fever** and **dysentery**.
• **Diarrhoea** means passing loose water many times a day; the body loses water and becomes weak.
• In **cholera** the stomach pains, the child vomits and passes watery stool again and again.
• **Dysentery** is diarrhoea with blood and pain in the belly.
• **Typhoid fever** brings long heat (fever), a bad head and a tummy that will not work well.
• Germs enter the body when we **drink dirty water**, **wash food in dirty water**, or touch food with
  hands that have not been washed after the toilet.
• The signs to watch for: **stomach pain**, **vomiting**, **loose stool**, **weakness**, **fever**.
• Prevention is better than cure: **boil or treat the water**, **wash hands with soap**, **cover the
  water**, **use the toilet**, and **wash the food** before it is eaten.
• A child with diarrhoea must **drink plenty of clean water** and the **ORS water** the clinic gives,
  and must be **taken to the clinic** the same day if the belly pain or fever does not stop.

**Words for my notebook**

Water-borne diseases — diarrhoea, cholera, typhoid and dysentery — come from dirty water. Clean water,
washed hands and covered containers keep us away from them.

**Let us talk**

1. What is the danger in drinking water straight from a stream?
2. Name one sign of a child who has diarrhoea.
3. When must we wash our hands with soap?
4. Which water is safer for drinking: boiled water or water from the barrel?


![A woman kneels beside a boy holding his tummy and offers him a covered cup, with a kettle boiling on a wood fire behind them.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-science/week2-clean-water-and-the-kettle.png)
**Worksheet**

1. A water-borne disease comes from A) new shoes B) a loud noise C) dirty water D) cold air
2. Which of these is a water-borne disease? A) ring worm B) toothache C) diarrhoea D) rash
3. To keep water safe for drinking we may A) keep it open B) boil it C) add sand D) leave it in the sun
4. The loose-water sickness with blood and belly pain is A) cough B) dysentery C) measles D) rash
5. Fill in: A child with diarrhoea must drink plenty of clean ____.
6. Mention three signs of a water-borne disease.
7. Write two sentences on what you do before you eat to keep germs away.

**My own work**

Draw a glass of clean covered water and a glass with dirt in it, then colour only the safe glass.

### WEEK 3 — TECHNOLOGY: SECURITY GADGETS THAT KEEP US SAFE

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Science and Technology · Third Term W4 (pp 497–502).*

**You will learn to**

• say what security means
• name gadgets that keep people and things safe
• tell what each gadget does
• do the right thing when an alarm sounds

**Things to know**

• **Security** means keeping **people** and **property** safe from thieves, fire and danger.
• A **security gadget** is a machine or a tool that helps people stay safe.
• **Locks and keys** keep doors and gates shut so that a stranger cannot walk in.
• A **burglar alarm** makes a loud sound when somebody forces a door or a window.
• A **CCTV camera** watches the gate or the shop and **records what happens**, so the police can see it.
• A **security light** turns bright at night so that nobody can move in the dark unseen.
• A **smoke alarm** warns the house when there is **fire or smoke**, long before the fire is big.
• A **security man** uses a **torch**, a **whistle**, a **radio**, a **uniform** and a **gate book** to
  know who is coming and going.
• When an alarm sounds: **leave the house at once**, go to the place your family has agreed on, and
  **tell a security person or a grown-up at the gate** — never hide under the bed and never go back for a toy.
• Gadgets help, but **they do not think**: a gate left open and a key under the mat undo a good lock.

**Words for my notebook**

Security gadgets — locks, alarms, cameras, lights and smoke detectors — help keep people and property
safe. When an alarm sounds we leave the house and tell the person at the gate.

**Let us talk**

1. What keeps a gate shut at night?
2. What does a camera on the wall of a shop do?
3. What sound does a smoke alarm make, and what do we do when we hear it?
4. Name one thing a security man carries.


![A camera on a pole above a padlocked gate, a bell button on the wall, and a torchlight and a whistle on a table under a shelter.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-science/week3-the-gate-and-the-camera.png)
**Worksheet**

1. Security means keeping people and property A) warm B) busy C) safe D) tall
2. Which gadget makes a loud sound when somebody forces a door? A) broom B) burglar alarm C) kettle D) ruler
3. A CCTV camera is used to A) cool the room B) pound yam C) water the lawn D) record what happens
4. The gadget that warns of fire and smoke is the A) smoke alarm B) doorbell C) scale D) torch
5. Fill in: When the alarm sounds we leave the house and tell the person at the ____.
6. Mention three security gadgets you can see in your street.
7. Write two sentences on what you do when the alarm rings at night.

**My own work**

Draw the gate of your house with its lock, then colour the gate and draw the key on a nail beside it.

---

### WEEK 4 — ANIMALS AND THE HOMES THEY LIVE IN

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Science and Technology · Third Term W7 (pp 497–502).*

**You will learn to**

• say that animals live in different places
• name the home of an animal
• tell a wild home from a home made by people
• say why an animal needs a home

**Things to know**

• Every animal has a **home** where it **sleeps, hides its young ones and stays safe**.
• Names we must know: **dog → kennel**, **chicken → coop**, **cow → shed or barn**, **horse → stable**,
  **bee → hive**, **bird → nest**, **lion → den**, **rabbit → burrow**, **ant → anthill**,
  **spider → web**, **fish → water**.
• A **nest** is built by the bird out of **twigs, grass, mud and feather**.
• A **web** is spun by the spider out of its own thread to catch small flies.
• An **anthill** is dug by ants under the ground; the loose soil is what we see heaped up outside.
• A **burrow** is a hole in the ground; the rabbit and the groundnut live in one.
• **Wild homes** are not made by people: the **forest**, the **cave**, the **river**, the **tree hole**,
  the **rock**.
• **Homes people make** are for the animals we keep: the **kennel**, the **coop**, the **shed**, the
  **stable**, the **aquarium**.
• An animal chooses or builds its home for **shade**, **safety**, **rest** and **young ones**.
• If a home is destroyed, the animal must **move, find food elsewhere or die** — that is why we do not
  burn bushes carelessly or cut every tree.

**Words for my notebook**

Animals live in homes: a dog in a kennel, a bird in a nest, a cow in a shed, a fish in water. Some homes
are made by nature; some are made by people who keep the animals.

**Let us talk**

1. Where does your neighbour's dog sleep at night?
2. What does a bird use to build its nest?
3. Which animal lives in a web?
4. Name an animal whose home is in the ground.


![A dog outside its kennel, a hen by a low poultry house, a beehive on a stand with bees near it, and a rat beside a round hole in the wall.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-science/week4-homes-of-the-animals.png)
**Worksheet**

1. The home of a chicken is called a A) den B) hive C) coop D) burrow
2. A spider lives in a A) nest B) web C) stable D) tank
3. The home of a cow is a A) barn or shed B) kennel C) nest D) hive
4. Which animal lives in an anthill? A) fish B) goat C) dog D) ant
5. Fill in: A rabbit lives in a hole in the ground called a ____.
6. Mention three animals whose homes are made by people.
7. Write the name of the home for each of these: bird, lion, fish, bee.

**My own work**

Draw six animals and their homes on one page, join each animal to its home with a line, then colour.

---

### WEEK 5 — ANIMALS AND THEIR YOUNG ONES

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Science and Technology · Third Term W8 (pp 497–502).*

**You will learn to**

• say that animals have young ones
• name the young one of an animal
• tell how a parent animal cares for its young
• match an animal to its young one

**Things to know**

• A **young one** is the baby of an animal; most young ones **look like their parents**, only smaller.
• The names we must know: **dog → puppy**, **cat → kitten**, **cow → calf**, **horse → foal**,
  **sheep → lamb**, **goat → kid**, **chicken → chick**, **duck → duckling**, **lion → cub**,
  **elephant → calf**, **bear → cub**.
• Some young ones do **not** look like the parent at first: the **frog's** young one is a **tadpole**
  that swims with a tail and has no legs; the **butterfly's** young one is a **caterpillar** that crawls.
• The growth from tadpole to frog and from caterpillar to butterfly is a **change of shape**.
• A mother animal gives **milk** to its young, keeps them **warm**, **cleans** them and **warns** them.
• A father animal may **guard** the young, as the dog guards the pups in the kennel.
• Birds **feed** the chick by carrying food in the mouth to the nest.
• The young one of a hen hatches from an **egg**; the young one of a cat is **born alive**.
• Animals that lay eggs: **hen, duck, tortoise, fish, snake, butterfly**. Animals that are born alive:
  **dog, cat, cow, goat, horse, rabbit**.
• We are kind to young animals: we do not chase them, we do not take them from the nest, and we do not
  tie a rope round a kid for play.

**Words for my notebook**

The young of a dog is a puppy, of a cat a kitten, of a hen a chick, of a cow a calf. Some, like the
tadpole and the caterpillar, change shape before they grow into the adult.

**Let us talk**

1. What do we call the baby of a hen?
2. Which animal's young one is called a kid?
3. Show me how a mother cat keeps her kittens warm.
4. What does a cow give its calf to drink?


![A hen with two chicks, a cow with her calf, a goat with her kid, and a cat with a kitten, all standing in a row.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-science/week5-mothers-and-their-young.png)
**Worksheet**

1. The young one of a goat is a A) kid B) lamb C) cub D) foal
2. A puppy is the young one of a A) hen B) dog C) cow D) bee
3. The young one of a butterfly is a A) pupa only B) chick C) caterpillar D) tadpole
4. Which of these animals hatches from an egg? A) horse B) dog C) duck D) goat
5. Fill in: The young one of a frog is called a ____.
6. Match four animals to their young ones by writing the name beside each.
7. Write two sentences on how a mother animal cares for her young ones.

**My own work**

Cut out pictures of animals and their young ones from an old paper, match them in pairs and paste each
pair on one line of your book.

---

### WEEK 6 — TECHNOLOGY: SIMPLE MACHINES USED IN THE HOME

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Science and Technology · Third Term W9 (pp 497–502).*

**You will learn to**

• say what a machine is
• name the simple machines used at home
• tell the work each one does
• handle a sharp tool safely

**Things to know**

• A **machine** is anything people make that **makes work easier, faster or better**.
• A **simple machine** has few parts and is moved by my own strength.
• In the kitchen: a **broom** sweeps the floor, a **cutlass** and a **knife** cut, a **spoon** and a
  **fork** carry food to the mouth, a **plate** holds the food, a **pot** cooks it.
• For the hands: a **bottle opener** and a **can opener** lift lids, a **hammer** knocks a nail, a
  **screwdriver** turns a screw, a **spanner** holds a bolt.
• For the body and the house: an **iron** presses clothes, a **fan** moves air, a **torch** gives
  light, a **bucket** carries water, a **wheelbarrow** carries loads.
• A machine makes work easier but it still needs **care**: a knife cuts the hand that holds it badly, a
  hot iron burns, a fan blade hurts a finger put inside it.
• Some machines are **for grown-ups to use**, and I only watch: the cutlass, the sharp knife, the hot
  iron, the blender that is plugged in.
• After use a tool is **cleaned, dried and put back in its place**; a knife is never left on the edge of
  a table and never carried about by its blade.
• Never touch a **plug, socket or wire** with **wet hands**, and never pull a cable to bring a machine down.
• Keeping tools in good order is part of using them: a blunt cutlass is more dangerous than a sharp one,
  because the hand slips.

**Words for my notebook**

A machine makes work easier. At home we use a broom, a knife, a spoon, a can opener, a hammer, an iron
and a fan. Every one of them needs care and a safe place.

**Let us talk**

1. Which machine in your house makes the clothes smooth?
2. What do we use to sweep the compound?
3. Which tools must a child not hold?
4. Where should a knife be kept after use?


![A plank on a stone lifts a heavy box, a boy pushes a loaded wheelbarrow, a plank leans up to a step as a ramp, and a bucket hangs from a rope at the well.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-science/week6-machines-at-home.png)
**Worksheet**

1. A machine is something that makes work A) harder B) easier C) longer D) wetter
2. Which tool is used to sweep the floor? A) spoon B) plate C) broom D) cup
3. A hammer is used to A) cut cloth B) drink water C) iron a shirt D) knock in a nail
4. Which of these is used for opening a tin of food? A) can opener B) broom C) basin D) mat
5. Fill in: We must never touch a plug or a wire with ____ hands.
6. Mention four simple machines you see in your kitchen.
7. Write two sentences on how you keep a sharp tool safe at home.

**My own work**

Draw four tools from your home in a row and colour the two that a child may hold up.

---

### WEEK 8 — TECHNOLOGY: SIMPLE MACHINES USED IN SCHOOL

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Science and Technology · Third Term W10 (pp 497–502).*

**You will learn to**

• name the simple machines we use in school
• tell what each one does for me
• use a sharp machine safely in class
• keep a school tool in good order

**Things to know**

• A **pencil sharpener** cuts the wood away so the lead comes out to a point; turn the pencil, not the
  blade.
• **Scissors** cut paper for art: hold the paper with the fingers **away** from the line the blade follows.
• A **stapler** joins two or three sheets with a small pin; press flat, never staple the desk.
• A **hole puncher** makes a round hole so the page can go into a file.
• A **ruler** measures in centimetres and draws a **straight line**; it is not a throwing stick.
• An **eraser** (a **duster** for the board) removes pencil marks; chalk and a board duster keep the
  board clean for the next writing.
• The **bell** or the **gong** calls the class in and out; one person rings it at the agreed time.
• A **dustpan and brush** carry the sweepings out of the room — the brush sweeps into the pan, never at
  another child.
• On the playground the **swing** and the **slide** are simple machines too: the swing is a **wheel and
  axle with a rope** and the slide is an **inclined plane** (a slope that saves the lifting).
• Every machine in the class is **school property**: it is carried with two hands, used for its own
  work, counted before it is returned to the cupboard, and **reported at once when it is damaged**.

**Words for my notebook**

At school I use a sharpener, scissors, a stapler, a hole puncher, a ruler, an eraser, a duster, a bell
and a dustpan. They are school things; I use them for their own work and return them in good order.

**Let us talk**

1. What do you use to make the pencil sharp again?
2. Which machine joins two sheets of paper?
3. Who rings the bell in our school?
4. What should we do when a scissors is broken?


![A girl pulls the rope at a tall pole, two boys sit on the ends of a seesaw on a stone, and a trolley carries a pile of books.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-science/week8-machines-in-school.png)
**Worksheet**

1. A pencil sharpener is used to A) cut paper B) make the pencil point C) clean the board D) carry books
2. Two sheets are joined together with a A) duster B) ruler C) stapler D) bell
3. Which machine makes a round hole in a page? A) hole puncher B) eraser C) brush D) chalk
4. The slide on our playground is a kind of machine called a A) lever B) wheel C) slope D) pulley
5. Fill in: A broken school tool must be ____ at once.
6. Mention four simple machines you use in the classroom.
7. Write two sentences on how you take care of one machine in your class.

**My own work**

Trace the straight edge of your ruler five times to make five lines, then colour the space between two of them.

---

### WEEK 9 — EXPLORING OUR ENVIRONMENT

*Depth: NERDC Primary 1 · Basic Science · First Term W1–2 (pp 624–625), the year's ending look at the
child's own surroundings.*

**You will learn to**

• say what my environment means
• name things I see, hear and smell around me
• describe my classroom, my compound and my street
• keep the place where I live clean

**Things to know**

• My **environment** is **everything around me**: the room I sit in, the compound, the street, the
  market, the church or mosque, the stream, the trees and the people.
• I learn about my environment with the **five senses**: I **see** colours and shapes, **hear** the
  seller's bell and the generator, **smell** cooking and dirty water, **touch** rough wall and smooth
  table, and sometimes **taste** the salt air or the dust.
• In the **classroom**: table, bench, board, duster, cupboard, fan, poster, my bag and my book.
• In the **school compound**: the block, the office, the toilet, the bin, the assembly ground, the
  playground, the water tank, the guard's chair.
• At **home**: the living room, the kitchen, the bedroom, the bathroom, the compound, the gutter, the
  bin, the gate.
• **People** are part of my environment too: my family, my teacher, my friends, my neighbours, the
  seller, the driver, the person who sweeps our street.
• Things that make an environment **unpleasant**: a blocked gutter, refuse in the gutter, a toilet with
  no water, a stream of dirty water, broken glass, an open drain.
• Things that make it **pleasant**: a swept compound, a covered bin, grass cut, trees planted, clean
  water, a painted wall, a classroom with light in it.
• On a **nature walk** I look, I listen, and I say what I found; I do not pick unknown fruit, and I do
  not put anything in my mouth.
• I can **keep my place clean**: refuse goes in the bin, the gutter is cleared, the tap is closed after
  use, and I wash my hands after the toilet.

**Words for my notebook**

My environment is everything around me — the room, the compound, the street, the people. I know it with
my five senses, and I help to keep it clean.

**Let us talk**

1. Name three things you can see from our classroom window.
2. What sound can you hear outside this room right now?
3. Which place in your compound must be kept cleanest, and why?
4. What did you find on our walk round the school?


![A path runs from a house past a tree, a stream and a market stall to a school building, with a boy walking along it holding a card.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-science/week9-my-way-to-school.png)
**Worksheet**

1. My environment means A) my exercise book B) everything around me C) only my family D) my shoes
2. Which sense tells me that water from the tap is cold? A) seeing B) hearing C) touching D) smelling
3. Refuse in the compound should be put A) in the gutter B) on the road C) in a covered bin D) under the bench
4. Which of these is part of my environment? A) the moon only B) my old shoe only C) nothing outside D) the seller in my street
5. Fill in: Everything around me that I can see, hear and smell is my ____.
6. Name four places inside your school compound.
7. Write two sentences on one thing you will do this week to keep your class clean.

**My own work**

Draw the map of your way from home to school with four things you pass on it, then colour the road.


---

# Cultural and Creative Arts (CCA)

Weeks 1 to 6, 8 and 9. Week 7 is Mid-Term Break, Week 10 Revision, Week 11 Examination,
Week 12 Closing — those weeks carry no note.
*Depth: NERDC 2025 · Nursery 3 · Creativity · Third Term, pp 572–575, and Primary 1 Cultural and
Creative Arts · First Term, pp 662–663 (taken two terms ahead, as the pack directs).*


### WEEK 1 — MAKING CARDS: PAPER THAT CARRIES A MESSAGE

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Creativity · Third Term W9 (pp 572–575).*

**You will learn to**

• say what a card is used for
• name four kinds of card
• fold a paper into a card
• write or copy a short message inside a card

**Things to know**

• A **card** is a small piece of stiff paper we **fold** and give to somebody to carry a **message**.
• A card says what the mouth finds hard to say: **thank you**, **I am sorry**, **well done**, **get
  well soon**.
• Kinds of card we know: a **birthday card**, a **thank-you card**, a **get-well card**, a
  **congratulations card**, an **invitation card** and a **holiday card**.
• We make the card by **folding** the paper once, in the middle, so it has a **front**, an **inside**
  and a **back**.
• The **front** carries a drawing or a picture; the **inside** carries the words.
• Words for the inside are kept **short**: "Happy birthday, Mama." "Thank you for the rice."
• We decorate with **crayon**, **colouring**, **cut-out paper**, **stickers**, a **ribbon** or a
  little **glitter**.
• Glitter and paste go on the **front**, never inside where the words are written.
• A card is **given with two hands** and is opened by the person it was written for.
• A card is art and speech together: the picture shows the feeling, the words say it.

**Words for my notebook**

A card is folded stiff paper that carries a short message and a picture. We make a birthday, a
thank-you or a get-well card, then write inside and decorate the front.

**Let us talk**

1. Whose birthday did you recently celebrate?
2. What would you write inside a get-well card?
3. Which part of the card carries the drawing — the front or the inside?
4. Show me how you fold one paper into a card.


![A girl holds open a folded card with a cake drawn on the front; scissors, a glue stick and a ribbon lie on the table.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/cultural-and-creative-arts/week1-making-a-card.png)
**Worksheet**

1. A card is made by ____ a stiff paper. A) tearing B) folding C) burning D) wetting
2. Which card do we send to a friend who is sick? A) birthday card B) invitation card C) holiday card D) get-well card
3. The words of a card are written on the ____ of it. A) inside B) back C) edge D) fold
4. A card carries a message and a ____ . A) price B) name of a shop C) picture D) key
5. Fill in: The words of a card are written on the ____ of it.
6. Mention two kinds of card you know.
7. Write two sentences on a card you made and the person you gave it to.

**My own work**

Fold a paper, draw a cake on the front and copy "Happy birthday" inside it.

---

### WEEK 2 — COLOUR SPLASH AND FEATHER ART

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Creativity · Third Term W10 (pp 572–575).*

**You will learn to**

• splash colour on paper with a brush
• control where the splash falls
• stick feathers flat on paper
• name a picture made with feathers

**Things to know**

• **Splash art** is colour thrown from a **wet brush** onto paper; the drops make marks no hand could draw.
• To splash, hold the brush **over** the paper and tap it gently with a finger — never at your neighbour.
• A **comb** or a toothbrush combed through the wet colour makes a **fine spray** of little dots.
• Cover the table with newspaper first; splash art goes where it is **meant** to go, not everywhere.
• More colour on the brush makes a **heavy** splash; a nearly dry brush makes a **light**, thin one.
• **Feather art** uses soft feathers stuck on paper with paste to make a picture.
• Feathers give a picture **texture** — it feels soft and raised, not flat like crayon.
• Pictures we can make from feathers: a **bird**, a **peacock** with a long tail, an **angel's wing**, a
  heap of **grass** in the wind.
• A feather stuck **on its edge** looks like a line; stuck **flat** it looks like a wing.
• Feathers are **natural things**, so we handle them gently and do not pull them from a living bird.

**Words for my notebook**

Splash art is colour tapped off a wet brush, and feather art is soft feathers pasted flat on paper to
give a picture texture and a raised edge.

**Let us talk**

1. What falls on the paper when you tap a wet brush?
2. How do we keep splash off the table and our clothes?
3. Does a feather feel smooth, rough or soft?
4. Which bird can we make from feathers on paper?


![A boy taps a toothbrush over paper so dots of paint fall on it, with three feathers and a jar of paste beside it.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/cultural-and-creative-arts/week2-splash-and-feathers.png)
**Worksheet**

1. We get a fine spray of colour by tapping a wet brush over a ____ . A) cup B) floor C) comb D) shoe
2. A picture with feathers feels ____ to the finger. A) soft and raised B) hot C) wet D) sharp
3. Feathers are best stuck on paper with ____ . A) water B) mud C) a stone D) paste
4. Heavy splash colour comes from a brush that is ____ . A) dry B) very wet with colour C) clean D) small
5. Fill in: Tapping a wet brush over paper makes ____ art.
6. Mention two pictures we can make with feathers on paper.
7. Write two sentences on how you kept the colour off the table.

**My own work**

Splash colour on one half of a paper, then paste three feathers on the other half to make a bird's tail.

---

### WEEK 3 — WHAT ART IS AND WHAT WE MAKE IT WITH

*Depth: NERDC Primary 1 · Cultural and Creative Arts · First Term W1 (pp 662–663).*

**You will learn to**

• say what art is
• name four kinds of art
• name the materials we use for art
• say how a picture can show a feeling

**Things to know**

• **Art** is making something **beautiful** or something that **shows what we feel or think**.
• Art is all around us: on **cloth**, on **walls**, on **pots**, on **doors**, on the **floor mats**.
• Kinds of art we do in class: **drawing**, **painting**, **colouring** and **craft** (making things by hand).
• Materials: **pencils**, **crayons**, **chalk**, **water colours**, **brushes**, **paper**, **cardboard**,
  **glue** and **scissors**.
• A **crayon** gives a soft thick line; a **pencil** gives a thin light one; **chalk** works on the floor.
• A **brush** is held near the metal ring, not at the end, so the hand can steer it.
• Colours used for a picture: **red**, **blue**, **yellow**, **green**, **brown**, **black**, **white**.
• A **smiling face** drawn big and round shows joy; a face with a dropped mouth shows sadness.
• Looking at an artwork means saying **what we see**, then **what it reminds us of**.
• Art is not only for the quick child; every finished picture in this class is kept and looked at.

**Words for my notebook**

Art is making beautiful things with our hands and eyes — drawing, painting, colouring and craft — with
pencils, crayons, brushes, colour, paper and scissors.

**Let us talk**

1. Point to something in this room that was made beautiful on purpose.
2. Which art material makes a soft thick line?
3. Draw a face that looks happy and a face that looks sorry.
4. What do you do with a brush before you dip it in colour?


![Crayons in a box, a brush in a jar, a folded paper fan and a clay pot on a shelf, with a girl holding up her drawing.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/cultural-and-creative-arts/week3-art-materials.png)
**Worksheet**

1. A picture drawn with a pencil is made with the hand and the ____ . A) nose B) feet C) ears D) eyes
2. Which of these is a craft? A) singing B) folding paper into a boat C) running D) sleeping
3. The material that gives a soft thick line is a ____ . A) ruler B) needle C) crayon D) spoon
4. To show joy we can draw a face that is ____ . A) sleeping B) crying C) blank D) smiling
5. Fill in: Drawing, painting, colouring and ____ are kinds of art.
6. Mention three materials you use for art.
7. Write two sentences on a picture you saw on cloth or on a wall.

**My own work**

Draw two faces on one paper — one happy, one sad — and colour the happy one with two bright colours.

---

### WEEK 4 — SCRIBBLING, LINES AND SHAPES

*Depth: NERDC Primary 1 · Cultural and Creative Arts · First Term W2 (pp 662–663).*

**You will learn to**

• scribble all over a paper and then inside a boundary
• draw straight, curved and zigzag lines
• draw a circle, a square and a triangle
• join dots to finish a picture

**Things to know**

• **Scribbling** is the hand moving the pencil freely; it makes the hand strong for writing.
• First we scribble **all over** the paper; then we scribble **only inside** a drawn ring.
• A **straight line** goes from one place to another without bending — like a piece of stick.
• A **curved line** bends like a smile, like a road going round a hill, like a banana.
• A **zigzag line** goes up and down sharply, like teeth, like the mountain in the distance.
• A **circle** is a round line whose ends meet; we say "one round line, no corner".
• A **square** has **four corners** and four like sides; a **triangle** has **three corners** and three sides.
• Things with a circle in them: a **plate**, a **ball**, the **sun**, a **clock**.
• Things with a square or a rectangle in them: a **window**, a **book**, a **mat**, a **box**.
• To **join dots**, the pencil starts at one dot and walks to the next without lifting off the paper.

**Words for my notebook**

We scribble first on the whole paper, then only inside a line. Straight, curved and zigzag lines make
circles, squares and triangles, and dots joined with no lift finish the picture.

**Let us talk**

1. Show me a zigzag line in the air with your finger.
2. How many corners does a triangle have?
3. Name two round things you saw on your way here.
4. Which is easier to draw, a circle or a square?


![A straight line, a curved line and a zigzag above a circle, a square and a triangle, with dots joined into a fish.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/cultural-and-creative-arts/week4-lines-and-shapes.png)
**Worksheet**

1. A line that goes up and down sharply is a ____ line. A) zigzag B) flat C) quiet D) long
2. A circle is a round line whose ends ____ . A) stay apart B) cross twice C) meet D) point
3. How many corners does a square have? A) one B) four C) two D) three
4. Before joining dots, the pencil must not ____ . A) touch paper B) hold chalk C) be black D) lift off
5. Fill in: A square has ____ corners and four like sides.
6. Mention one round thing and one square thing you saw today.
7. Write two sentences on the lines you drew — straight, curved and zigzag.

**My own work**

Fill one paper with scribble, then draw a big circle, a square and a triangle inside it and colour only the triangle.

---

### WEEK 5 — MAKING LETTERS AND NUMBERS BEAUTIFUL

*Depth: NERDC Primary 1 · Cultural and Creative Arts · First Term W3 (pp 662–663).*

**You will learn to**

• draw a big letter and colour its inside
• decorate a letter with dots and lines
• make a pattern using numbers
• paste paper bits onto a letter

**Things to know**

• A letter can be **art** as well as writing: we draw it **big** and leave it **hollow** to colour in.
• To make a hollow letter, trace a big letter twice, once beside itself, then join the tops and the feet.
• We decorate the outline with **dots**, **small circles**, **zigzags** or **little leaves** drawn on it.
• Colour stays **inside** the letter; the line of the letter is left whole and dark.
• Numbers can be decorated too: **1** with a hat, **2** with a boat's sail, **8** with two round plates.
• A **pattern** is marks that **repeat**: dot, line, dot, line — or 1, 2, 1, 2, 1, 2.
• A **collage** letter is made by **pasting** small bits of coloured paper, seeds or beads on the letter.
• Paste is spread **thin** with a finger or a stick, and the paper is **pressed flat**, not rubbed.
• Our own name is the best letter to decorate, because we can read every one of its letters.
• Artistic writing means the letter is still **readable** after we have made it beautiful.

**Words for my notebook**

We draw big hollow letters and numbers, colour inside them, decorate the edge with dots and lines, and
paste paper bits to make a pattern that can still be read.

**Let us talk**

1. Which letter of your name is the roundest?
2. What repeats in the pattern: dot, line, dot, line?
3. Show me how you press a pasted paper bit flat.
4. Which number looks like two plates on top of each other?


![A boy presses torn bits of paper onto a big star outline, with a jar of paste and a heap of paper scraps beside him.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/cultural-and-creative-arts/week5-paper-collage.png)
**Worksheet**

1. A hollow letter is one we can ____ inside. A) tear B) colour C) fold D) erase
2. Dots on a line of a letter are there to make it ____ . A) heavy B) wet C) beautiful D) new
3. A pattern of dot, line, dot, line is made of marks that ____ . A) melt B) disappear C) stand still D) repeat
4. A letter covered with pasted paper bits is called a ____ . A) collage B) song C) plate D) dream
5. Fill in: A pattern is made of marks that ____ .
6. Mention two ways to decorate the line of a letter.
7. Write two sentences on the letter you decorated and the colour you used.

**My own work**

Draw the first letter of your name big and hollow, colour inside it, then put dots all along its line.

---

### WEEK 6 — COLOURS: THE THREE THAT MAKE THE OTHERS

*Depth: NERDC Primary 1 · Cultural and Creative Arts · First Term W4 and W7 (pp 662–663).*

**You will learn to**

• name the three primary colours
• name the three secondary colours
• mix two colours to get a new one
• point to colours in our surroundings

**Things to know**

• A **colour** is what makes things look **red**, **blue**, **yellow** or **green** to the eye.
• The **primary colours** are **red**, **blue** and **yellow**; no two colours can be mixed to make them.
• Two primary colours mixed give a **secondary colour**.
• **Red + yellow = orange** (the colour of a ripe palm fruit and of sunset).
• **Yellow + blue = green** (the colour of a fresh leaf and of the grass).
• **Red + blue = purple** (the colour of a ripe plum and of some beans).
• Other colours we use: **black**, **white**, **brown**, **pink** and **grey**.
• In our surroundings: the **sky** is blue, the **grass** is green, the **sun** is yellow, a **ripe tomato**
  is red, the **soil** after rain is brown.
• Much red with a little white makes **pink**; much black with a little white makes **grey**.
• Before two colours are mixed on paper, the brush is **rinsed** in the water cup, or the mix turns muddy.

**Words for my notebook**

Red, blue and yellow are primary colours. Mixing two of them gives orange, green or purple, and the rest
of the colours we see are around us in the sky, the leaves and the soil.

**Let us talk**

1. What colour is the sky at noon today?
2. Which two colours must I mix to get green?
3. Point to something brown in this room.
4. What colour is a ripe orange — the fruit?


![A girl mixes paint in a small dish with a brush; three open jars, their lids and a cup of water stand in a row.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/cultural-and-creative-arts/week6-mixing-colours.png)
**Worksheet**

1. Which colour is a primary colour? A) green B) orange C) purple D) yellow
2. Red and yellow mixed together give ____ . A) green B) orange C) black D) blue
3. The colour of a fresh leaf is ____ . A) green B) white C) red D) grey
4. How many primary colours are there? A) one B) eight C) three D) two
5. Fill in: Red and blue mixed together give ____ .
6. Mention the three primary colours.
7. Write two sentences on colours you saw outside on your way to school.

**My own work**

Colour one leaf green, one orange and one purple, then draw a ring round each colour you mixed yourself.

---

### WEEK 8 — CRAFTS: MAKING THINGS WITH OUR HANDS

*Depth: NERDC Primary 1 · Cultural and Creative Arts · First Term W8 (pp 662–663).*

**You will learn to**

• say what a craft is
• name three kinds of craft
• fold paper into halves and quarters
• carry and use scissors safely

**Things to know**

• A **craft** is a thing we **make with our hands**, usually something we can use or wear.
• Crafts we know in class: **paper craft**, **clay modelling** and **bead work**.
• Materials for craft: **paper**, **cardboard**, **glue**, **scissors**, **clay**, **thread**, **beads**,
  **old bottle caps**.
• Folding a paper once, corner to corner or edge to edge, gives **two halves**; folding again gives
  **four quarters**.
• A crease is made by running a **fingernail** along the fold, so the paper keeps the shape.
• A **paper boat**, a **hat** and an **aeroplane** are all made from one folded paper.
• **Clay modelling**: roll clay on the table into a **snake**, then coil it into a **pot**; keep the clay
  cool and covered with a damp cloth.
• **Bead work**: beads are threaded on a strong thread, one after another, to make a **necklace** or a
  **bracelet**.
• Scissors are carried with the **blades closed** and the **handles forward** to the person you hand them to.
• Scissors **cut paper and cloth**; they do not cut hair, thread on a garment, or a neighbour's work.

**Words for my notebook**

A craft is a thing made with our hands: paper folding, clay modelling and bead work. We fold halves and
quarters, press every crease, and carry scissors closed with the handles forward.

**Let us talk**

1. What did you make with clay last time?
2. How many quarters do you get when a paper is folded twice?
3. Which part of the scissors do you hold when you pass them to a friend?
4. Name one thing that scissors must never cut.


![A boy folds a paper in half; a paper boat, a coil of clay, a bead necklace and a pair of scissors lie on the table.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/cultural-and-creative-arts/week8-paper-clay-beads.png)
**Worksheet**

1. A craft is a thing made with our ____ . A) ears B) hands C) toes D) teeth
2. Folding a paper once, edge to edge, gives two ____ . A) cups B) birds C) halves D) rings
3. A coiled snake of clay can become a ____ . A) pot B) song C) shadow D) colour
4. We carry scissors to a friend with the ____ forward. A) blades B) points C) paper D) fingers
5. Fill in: Folding a paper once, edge to edge, gives two ____ .
6. Mention three things we use for craft work.
7. Write two sentences on how scissors are carried and used safely.

**My own work**

Fold a paper into four quarters, cut one edge into fringes, then open it to see a paper fan.

---

### WEEK 9 — DECORATION: MAKING PLAIN THINGS BEAUTIFUL

*Depth: NERDC Primary 1 · Cultural and Creative Arts · First Term W10 (pp 662–663).*

**You will learn to**

• say what decoration means
• make a paper flower and a garland
• draw a border pattern round a picture
• make a decorated name tag

**Things to know**

• **Decoration** means making a plain thing **beautiful** by adding colours, patterns or shapes to it.
• We decorate the **classroom walls**, our **notice board**, our **books**, our **name tags** and the **corners** of a drawing.
• A **pattern** repeats: dots, then lines, then small circles; a **border** is a pattern that runs round the edge.
• A **paper flower** is made by cutting **petals** round a circle, folding it, and pressing a **button centre** in it.
• A **garland** is made by stringing **paper cups**, **leaves** or **beads** one after another on a thread.
• For a garland, two **shapes** are placed back to back and the thread runs between them.
• Our country's colours, **green** and **white**, can be used in a garland for a school day.
• A **name tag** carries the name written **big and clear**, with a pattern round the edge.
• Decoration must not **cover what we need to see**: the name stays readable, the border stays outside it.
• Everything used for decoration is **what we find**: paper, leaves, seeds, old cloth, caps, string.

**Words for my notebook**

Decoration makes plain things beautiful. We repeat a pattern to draw a border, cut paper flowers, string
a garland of paper cups, and write our name big on a tag with a pattern round the edge.

**Let us talk**

1. Which wall in this room could use a decoration?
2. What shape do we cut for a paper flower's petals?
3. What repeats in the border you drew today?
4. Why must a name tag still be easy to read?


![A garland of paper cups and leaves hangs above a paper flower and a girl holding a blank tag with a dotted border.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/cultural-and-creative-arts/week9-garland-and-tag.png)
**Worksheet**

1. Decoration makes a plain thing ____ . A) beautiful B) heavier C) wetter D) quieter
2. A pattern that runs round the edge of a drawing is a ____ . A) song B) border C) floor D) shadow
3. A garland is made by stringing cups or beads on a ____ . A) stone B) needle C) thread D) plate
4. On a name tag, the name must still be ____ . A) hidden B) folded C) erased D) readable
5. Fill in: A pattern that runs round the edge of a drawing is a ____ .
6. Mention two things we decorate in this class.
7. Write two sentences on the name tag you made and the pattern on its edge.

**My own work**

Draw your name big, then put a border of dots and lines all round it and colour two corners green.


---

# Christian Religious Studies (CRS)

Weeks 1 to 6, 8 and 9. Week 7 is Mid-Term Break, Week 10 Revision, Week 11 Examination,
Week 12 Closing — those weeks carry no note.
*Depth: NERDC 2025 · Primary 2 · Christian Religious Studies · First Term, pp 699–701, and Nursery 3
· CRS · Third Term (Jesus our Saviour, pp 576–578), taken from the pack's off-by-three mapping.*


### WEEK 1 — JESUS CHRIST OUR SAVIOUR

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · CRS · Third Term (pp 576–578).*

**You will learn to**

• say what a saviour is
• tell why Jesus came into the world
• say what Jesus did for me on the cross
• tell the Easter story in my own words

**Things to know**

• A **saviour** is somebody who **saves** us or **rescues** us out of danger.
• A **sin** is the wrong thing we do — **fighting**, **lying**, **stealing**, refusing to obey.
• Everybody does wrong things sometimes; the Bible says **all have sinned** (Romans 3:23).
• Sin puts a **wall** between us and God, because God is holy and cannot be pleased with wrong.
• We cannot pull the wall down ourselves; **Jesus came to take it down for us**.
• Jesus **died on the cross** for us. He did no sin, yet He took the punishment that was ours.
• His death shows **how much God loves us**: God so loved the world that He gave His only Son (John 3:16).
• On the **third day** Jesus **rose from the dead** — the women came and the stone was rolled away.
• Jesus is **alive in heaven today**, near God the Father, and He still hears us when we speak.
• Because Jesus died and rose again, we can be called **God's children** and **live with God for ever**.
• To **accept** Jesus is to say: *You are my Saviour; I will follow You.*

**Words for my notebook**

A saviour is one who saves. We all do wrong, and sin puts a wall between us and God, so Jesus died on
the cross for us and rose again on the third day that we may be God's children.

**Let us talk**

1. What does the word saviour mean?
2. Name one wrong thing the Bible calls sin.
3. On which day did Jesus rise from the dead?
4. What do you say when you accept Jesus as your Saviour?


![A cross on a hill beside an open tomb with its stone rolled away and the sun rising.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/christian-religious-studies/week1-cross-and-empty-tomb.png)
**Worksheet**

1. A saviour is somebody who ____ us. A) frightens B) saves C) forgets D) paints
2. Jesus died on the ____ for us. A) hill B) road C) river bank D) cross
3. Jesus rose from the dead on the ____ day. A) third B) seventh C) tenth D) second
4. Because Jesus died and rose again, we can be ____ . A) kings of the earth B) rich men C) owners of land D) children of God
5. Fill in: A sin is the ____ thing we do.
6. Mention two wrong things the Bible calls sin.
7. Write two sentences on why Jesus came into the world.

**My own work**

Draw three hills with a cross on the middle hill, then draw the sun rising behind it.

---

### WEEK 2 — JESUS CALLS US FRIENDS, BROTHERS AND SISTERS

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · CRS · Third Term (pp 576–578).*

**You will learn to**

• say what Jesus calls us
• tell who my brothers and sisters in God's family are
• show a friend what love looks like
• say how we prove that we are Jesus' friends

**Things to know**

• Jesus said, "**You are My friends**" (John 15:15) — He did not call those who follow Him strangers.
• A **friend** is one who stays near, tells the truth, shares and does not hurt you.
• Jesus is a friend who **never leaves**, even when other children go home.
• In God's family, everybody in the class is my **brother or my sister**.
• That is why we do not say "that child is not my relation, I will not help him".
• Jesus wants us to **love one another**; this is the sign by which people will know us.
• We show that we are Jesus' friends by **obeying** what He taught, not only by singing.
• A friend is known in what he **does**: sharing a bench, giving a drink, carrying a heavy book.
• Little children can be Jesus' friends too — He did not wait for us to grow tall.
• When I hurt my friend, I go back and say **"I am sorry"**, because friends do not keep anger.

**Words for my notebook**

Jesus calls us friends and brothers and sisters in God's family. We prove we are His friends by loving
and obeying one another, and by saying sorry when we hurt a friend.

**Let us talk**

1. What does Jesus call the ones who follow Him?
2. Who is your brother or sister in God's family?
3. What did you do for a friend today?
4. How do we show that we are Jesus' friends?


![children-in-a-ring](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/christian-religious-studies/week2-children-in-a-ring.png)
**Worksheet**

1. Jesus said, "I call you ____ " (John 15:15). A) servants only B) strangers C) friends D) visitors
2. In God's family, everybody in the class is my ____ . A) brother or sister B) paying guest C) neighbour far away D) class enemy
3. A friend is one who ____ . A) hides my exercise book B) hurts me C) laughs at me D) stays near and tells the truth
4. We show that we are Jesus' friends by ____ . A) forgetting Him B) obeying what He taught C) teasing the small ones D) sleeping in church
5. Fill in: When I hurt my friend, I go and say "____" .
6. Mention three things a friend does for you.
7. Write two sentences on the friend you thank God for.

**My own work**

Draw four children holding hands in a ring, then draw a small lamb sitting inside the ring.

---

### WEEK 3 — JESUS COMMANDS US TO LOVE

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · CRS · Third Term (pp 576–578).*

**You will learn to**

• say the greatest commandment of the law
• tell who my neighbour is
• show love to God in three ways
• show love to people by what I do

**Things to know**

• The **greatest commandment**: **love the Lord your God with all your heart** (Matthew 22:37).
• The **second** is like it: **love your neighbour as yourself** (Matthew 22:39).
• Jesus said, "**Love one another as I have loved you**" (John 13:34).
• Love is the **most important** thing Jesus taught; all other teaching hangs on these two.
• We show love to God by **praying**, by **thanking** Him, by **worshipping** and by **obeying**.
• **Who is my neighbour?** Everybody around me — my family, my class, my teacher, the baby at home.
• My neighbour is also the **stranger** at the gate and the child who is **different** from me.
• Love is a **verb**: helping a friend up, sharing an orange, giving water to the old woman.
• Love does not **push**, does not **scold**, does not **take** what is not mine.
• A kind word is love; a kept promise is love; a divided biscuit is love.
• It is easy to love those who love me; Jesus asks me to love even the child who annoys me.

**Words for my notebook**

Jesus gave two great commands: love God with all my heart and love my neighbour as myself. My neighbour
is everybody around me, and love is known by what I do — helping, sharing and being kind.

**Let us talk**

1. Which is the greatest commandment of the law?
2. Who counts as your neighbour?
3. Name one way you showed love at home this week.
4. How do we show love to God without leaving this class?


![love-in-what-we-do](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/christian-religious-studies/week3-love-in-what-we-do.png)
**Worksheet**

1. The greatest commandment is to love ____ . A) my ball B) my teacher only C) the market D) God with all my heart
2. The second commandment is to love ____ . A) my neighbour as myself B) only my brothers C) nobody D) only those who give me things
3. Love is mostly known by what we ____ . A) dream B) hide C) do D) own
4. "Love one another as I have loved you" were words of ____ . A) Jesus B) Pharaoh C) my classmate D) the soldier
5. Fill in: My neighbour is everybody ____ me.
6. Mention four people you must show love to.
7. Write two sentences on a kind thing you did for somebody different from you.

**My own work**

Draw a boy helping a small girl to stand up, then draw two children sharing one orange between them.

---

### WEEK 4 — JESUS WANTS US TO OBEY GOD'S COMMANDMENTS

*Depth: NERDC Primary 2 · CRS · First Term (pp 699–701), and Exodus 20 in child's words.*

**You will learn to**

• say what a commandment is
• name five of God's rules for living
• tell why we obey God
• say who brought God's rules to the people

**Things to know**

• **God's rules for living are called commandments** — they are not punishments, they are guidance.
• God gave His rules through **Moses** on the mountain; they were written on **two tables of stone**.
• We keep them in our hearts: **love God only**, **do not make an image to worship**, **do not steal**, **do not lie**, **do not be jealous of what others have**, and **give honour to father and mother**.
• We obey God because **He loves us** and **knows what is best** for us.
• A child who obeys keeps himself out of a great deal of sorrow.
• **Jesus obeyed God perfectly** all His life, even when obeying was painful.
• Jesus did not obey only when He felt like it; that is what "perfectly" means.
• Obeying God **makes us happy** and **pleases Him**, like a good song pleases the one who hears it.
• We cannot obey in our own power: we **ask Jesus to help us** obey every morning.
• To **lie**, to **take what is not mine**, to **hit a smaller child** — each one breaks a rule we know.

**Words for my notebook**

Commandments are God's rules for living, given through Moses on two tables of stone. We obey because God
loves us and knows what is best, and Jesus obeyed God perfectly so that we may learn from Him.

**Let us talk**

1. What do we call God's rules for living?
2. Who brought the commandments to the people?
3. Which rule keeps my mouth true?
4. Why is it good to obey God?


![two-tables-of-stone](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/christian-religious-studies/week4-two-tables-of-stone.png)
**Worksheet**

1. God's rules for living are called ____ . A) commandments B) birthday gifts C) market prices D) class songs
2. The commandments were given through ____ on the mountain. A) Pharaoh B) Moses C) the soldier D) my neighbour
3. Which of these is one of God's rules? A) take what you like B) always sleep late C) do not lie D) hide your book
4. We obey God because He ____ . A) is far away B) cannot see us C) lives in the sky only D) loves us and knows what is best
5. Fill in: Jesus obeyed God ____ , even when it was painful.
6. Mention three of God's rules you learnt this week.
7. Write two sentences on a time you chose to obey instead of doing wrong.

**My own work**

Draw two plain stone tables standing side by side on a mountain, and a small boy kneeling in front of them.

---

### WEEK 5 — THE MEANING OF PRAYER

*Depth: NERDC Primary 2 · CRS · First Term W1–2, prayer (pp 699–701).*

**You will learn to**

• say what prayer is
• name four places I can pray
• tell who listens when I pray
• pray in two different ways

**Things to know**

• **Prayer is talking to God** — with my mouth, or only in my heart where nobody can hear.
• Prayer is like talking to a **father** or a **friend**: I say what I did, what I need and what I thank for.
• **God listens** when I pray; He is not far and He is not busy.
• **God loves** it when His little children speak to Him, and He does not mind small words.
• I can pray **anywhere**: at home, in class, in the church, on the playground, in the bus.
• I can pray **any time**: in the morning, in the afternoon, at night, and between two lessons.
• We pray **out loud** when we are together, and **silently** in our hearts when we are many.
• We also pray by **singing** to God, and by **reading** the prayers He gave us in the Bible.
• **Folding the hands** and **closing the eyes** are not what makes God hear; they help me to think on Him.
• Nobody has to be tall to pray: **a child's short prayer is heard** the same as the pastor's long one.
• Prayer is our **conversation** with God — He speaks to me in His word, I speak to Him in prayer.

**Words for my notebook**

Prayer is talking to God like talking to a father or a friend. I can pray anywhere and any time, out loud
or in my heart, and God listens to me.

**Let us talk**

1. What is prayer?
2. Where did you pray yesterday?
3. Who listens when you pray?
4. What is the difference between praying out loud and praying in your heart?


![praying-by-the-bed](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/christian-religious-studies/week5-praying-by-the-bed.png)
**Worksheet**

1. Prayer is talking to ____ . A) my friend B) God C) the door D) my toy
2. God hears my prayer because He ____ . A) is far away B) cannot hear small ones C) is busy with angels D) listens to me
3. Which of these is a way to pray? A) singing to God B) hiding under a bed C) counting my beads D) shouting at friends
4. Folding my hands and closing my eyes help me to ____ . A) find my shoes B) sleep well C) think on God D) warm my hands
5. Fill in: We can pray at home, in class, in the church and on the ____ .
6. Mention four times of the day I am allowed to pray.
7. Write two sentences on what you thanked God for this morning.

**My own work**

Draw a girl kneeling beside her bed with her hands folded, and a bird sitting on the window sill.

---

### WEEK 6 — WHEN TO PRAY

*Depth: NERDC Primary 2 · CRS · First Term W3–4, when to pray (pp 699–701).*

**You will learn to**

• name the special times we pray every day
• say what we ask God in the morning and at night
• thank God before a meal
• tell when else it is good to pray

**Things to know**

• There is **no wrong time** to pray; we can pray any time we want.
• In the **morning** we thank God for the night that kept us safe, and we ask for **protection** for the day.
• **Before a meal** we thank God for the food; that short prayer is called **saying grace**.
• At **night** we thank God for the day and ask for **peaceful sleep**.
• **Before school** we ask God for wisdom to understand and safety on the road.
• When we are **happy**, we pray and thank God; when we are **sad or afraid**, we ask for help and comfort.
• When somebody is **sick**, we pray for their healing; when a friend is hurting, we pray with them.
• **Before a class test** we ask God to open our minds, not to give us another child's work.
• The Bible says, "**Pray without ceasing**" (1 Thessalonians 5:17) — that means keep talking to God all day.
• A child who prays at these four times — **morning, food, school, bed** — is learning to keep God near.
• Praying **with the family** at home is good, and praying **alone** is good too.

**Words for my notebook**

We pray in the morning, before a meal, before school and at night. We also pray when we are happy, sad,
afraid, or when somebody is sick, for the Bible says we should pray without ceasing.

**Let us talk**

1. What do you thank God for in the morning?
2. What do we say before we eat?
3. Name one other time it is good to pray.
4. What does "pray without ceasing" mean for a child?


![four-times-to-pray](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/christian-religious-studies/week6-four-times-to-pray.png)
**Worksheet**

1. In the morning we thank God for the ____ . A) night that kept us B) rain in the farm C) market on Sunday D) lost shoe
2. The short prayer we say before a meal is called ____ . A) church bell B) roll call C) saying grace D) day break
3. At night we ask God for ____ . A) another plate of food B) a long class C) a new cap D) peaceful sleep
4. When somebody is sick we ____ . A) laugh at them B) play louder C) leave the house D) pray for their healing
5. Fill in: The Bible says, "Pray without ____ ."
6. Mention four times we should pray in a single day.
7. Write two sentences on the last time you prayed before you ate.

**My own work**

Draw four small pictures in a row: the rising sun, a bowl of food, a school cap at a gate, and the moon in a window.

---

### WEEK 8 — HOW TO PRAY

*Depth: NERDC Primary 2 · CRS · First Term W1–2, how to pray (pp 699–701).*

**You will learn to**

• show three positions we can pray in
• tell why we fold the hands
• pray a short prayer with the class
• say what Jesus did when He prayed

**Things to know**

• We may pray **standing**, **sitting**, **kneeling** or simply with the **head bowed**.
• **Kneeling and bowing the head** are ways of showing God honour, not rules that make the prayer work.
• Jesus Himself **knelt** when He prayed in the garden (Luke 22:41).
• **Folded hands** keep the fingers from fidgeting; **closed eyes** keep the mind from wandering.
• When we pray **together**, one voice speaks and the rest say **"Amen"** at the end, meaning *so be it*.
• A prayer can be as **short** as "Thank You, God, for my mother", and as **long** as a whole song.
• We **thank** first, then we **ask** for what we need, then we **ask God to wash away** the wrong we did.
• It is right to **pray for somebody else**: my friend who is sick, my teacher, my father and mother.
• Prayer is not only words from the mouth; the **heart** must go with the mouth.
• After praying, I **listen** quietly for a moment, because God also speaks to me through His word.
• Do not **copy another child's prayer** in a loud voice; God is not hearing the class, He is hearing you.

**Words for my notebook**

We can pray standing, sitting or kneeling, with folded hands and bowed head. We thank God first, then
ask, then own our wrong, and we may pray for other people as well as for ourselves.

**Let us talk**

1. Show me a position you like to pray in.
2. Why do we fold our hands when we pray?
3. What do we say at the end of a prayer together?
4. Put your own short prayer in this order: thank, ask, own what you did wrong.


![three-prayer-positions](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/christian-religious-studies/week8-three-prayer-positions.png)
**Worksheet**

1. Which of these is a position we may pray in? A) kneeling B) swimming C) running D) dancing
2. We fold our hands and close our eyes to help us to ____ . A) find a seat B) hide from friends C) fix our minds on God D) keep warm
3. When the class finishes praying we say ____ . A) good night B) sleep well C) the class is through D) Amen
4. In this class we pray first to ____ God. A) thank B) fool C) frighten D) pay
5. Fill in: Jesus ____ when He prayed in the garden.
6. Mention three positions of the body used in prayer.
7. Say and write your own two-sentence prayer for a friend who is sick.

**My own work**

Draw three children praying: one standing, one kneeling with the head bowed, one sitting with an open book.

---

### WEEK 9 — PRAYING EVERY DAY

*Depth: NERDC Primary 2 · CRS · First Term W3–4, keeping on in prayer (pp 699–701).*

**You will learn to**

• keep a daily order of prayer
• use the pattern Jesus taught
• pray for four kinds of people
• tell how God speaks back to my prayer

**Things to know**

• A **daily order** keeps prayer from being forgotten: **morning**, **before food**, **before school**, **bed**.
• Jesus taught His friends a pattern: "**Our Father in heaven**, may Your name be honoured; give us our
  food today; forgive us as we forgive others; keep us from evil" (Matthew 6:9–13).
• That pattern has four parts: **praise God**, **ask for daily needs**, **own wrong and forgive**, **ask for keeping**.
• We pray for the **sick**, for our **parents and guardians**, for our **teachers and the class**, and for our
  **country** and its leaders.
• We also pray for the person who **annoys** us; that is the hardest and the best prayer a child can say.
• God speaks to us **through the Bible**, through a good thought, and through what a grown-up tells us.
• God replies in three kinds of way: **"yes"**, **"wait"**, and **"I have something better"**.
• A prayer is not better because it is **long**; the widow's two coins were smaller than the rich man's
  basket, yet they were noticed.
• Keeping a small **mark on a paper chart** each morning is a way to see the order grow — one tick a day.
• If I forget a time, I do not start all over in shame; I simply **begin again at the next time**.

**Words for my notebook**

I keep a daily order of prayer — morning, before food, before school and at bed — using the pattern Jesus
taught: praise God, ask for needs, own my wrong and forgive, and ask to be kept. God replies yes, wait,
or something better.

**Let us talk**

1. Which part of your day will you never leave without a prayer?
2. What are the four parts of the pattern Jesus taught?
3. Name somebody outside this class you will pray for tonight.
4. What do you do when God's reply to your prayer is "wait"?


![a-book-and-a-day](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/christian-religious-studies/week9-a-book-and-a-day.png)
**Worksheet**

1. The pattern of prayer Jesus taught begins with "Our ____ in heaven." A) Father B) teacher C) king D) neighbour
2. Which of these is not one of the four parts of that pattern? A) praising God B) asking for my daily bread C) counting my toys D) asking to be kept from evil
3. We should also pray for the person who ____ us. A) annoys B) loves C) calls D) sees
4. God's reply to a prayer may be yes, wait, or ____ . A) never B) something better C) go away D) sleep on it
5. Fill in: A prayer is not better because it is ____ .
6. Mention four kinds of people we should pray for.
7. Write the four times you will mark on your own prayer chart for tomorrow.

**My own work**

Draw a sun at one end of a line, a bowl of food in the middle and a moon with two stars at the other end, then put a tick under each.


---

# Basic Digital Literacy

Weeks 1 to 6, 8 and 9. Week 7 is Mid-Term Break, Week 10 Revision, Week 11 Examination,
Week 12 Closing — those weeks carry no note.
*Depth: NERDC 2025 · Nursery 3 · Basic Digital Literacy · Third Term — the pack puts one internal part of
the computer on each week (headphones and care, CPU, RAM, hard drive, motherboard, power supply, cooling,
how the parts work together), with the same pool of input and output devices across all of them.*


### WEEK 1 — HEADPHONES, AND HOW WE CARE FOR A MACHINE

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Digital Literacy · Third Term W1.*

**You will learn to**

• say what headphones are for
• wear headphones the right way
• name two machines that use headphones
• say three rules for caring for a computer

**Things to know**

• **Headphones** are two small sound cups on a band; they put sound **into the ears** and nowhere else.
• We use headphones to hear a story, a song or a lesson **without disturbing the rest of the class**.
• Put the band **over the top of the head**, then let the cups **sit on the ears**; never push them inside.
• **Sound kept low** is how a child hears a long time; loud sound makes the ear tired and can hurt it.
• One headphone **for one child** — we do not share the cup that sat on another child's ear.
• Headphones are used with a **computer**, a **phone**, a **radio** and a **tablet**.
• The **wire** is kept free from pulling, from the wheel of a chair and from being stepped on.
• We care for a machine by keeping **food and water away** from it and by **washing the hands** before use.
• A screen is wiped with a **soft dry cloth**, never with a wet one and never with a pencil tip.
• We **hold a keyboard with two hands** when we carry it, and we put the chair back under the table.

**Words for my notebook**

Headphones put sound into our ears and keep it out of the class. We wear them low and clean, we keep food
and water from the machine, and we wipe a screen only with a soft dry cloth.

**Let us talk**

1. What do we hear with on a computer?
2. Why is a low sound better than a loud one?
3. What must stay away from a keyboard and a screen?
4. Show me how you put the band of a headphone over your head.


![A girl sits at a table wearing big headphones with one hand near her ear, with a blank screen, a keyboard and a box on the table, and an open cup of water kept far away on another stool.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-digital-literacy/week1-headphones-and-care.png)
**Worksheet**

1. Headphones put sound into our ____ . A) nose B) hair C) ears D) shoes
2. Which of these is used with headphones? A) a computer B) a broom C) a cutlass D) a pot
3. A screen is wiped with a ____ . A) wet rag B) rough sand C) pencil tip D) soft dry cloth
4. Before we touch a keyboard we wash our ____ . A) feet B) hands C) ears D) knees
5. Fill in: Loud sound makes the ear ____ and can hurt it.
6. Mention three things we keep away from a computer.
7. Write two sentences on how you wear a headphone the right way.

**My own work**

Draw a pair of headphones with a band and two round cups, then draw a child wearing them with one hand up to an ear.

---

### WEEK 2 — THE CPU: THE PART THAT THINKS

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Digital Literacy · Third Term W2.*

**You will learn to**

• say what the CPU is
• tell what the CPU does for the whole machine
• name the box where the CPU lives
• say why we do not open the box

**Things to know**

• The **CPU** is the part of the computer that **does the thinking**; it is like the **head** of a person.
• When you press a key, the news runs to the **CPU**, and the CPU decides what the screen will show.
• CPU means **Central Processing Unit** — the words a big man uses; we call it **the thinking part**.
• The CPU lives inside the **big box** under the screen, called the **system unit** or the computer's body.
• The box has **small holes** for air; the CPU gets warm when it works hard, like a leg gets warm when it runs.
• We **do not open the box** and we do not put a finger or a pencil inside it; there is power in there.
• Inside the box the CPU sits on a **flat board** with its little brothers: **RAM** and the **hard drive**.
• Other machines think too: a **phone**, a **television**, a **radio** and a **pump** all have a small CPU.
• A computer with a tired CPU is like a child with no sleep — it works **slowly**.
• We keep the box **on a table**, not on the floor, so that dust and water stay away from it.

**Words for my notebook**

The CPU is the thinking part of a computer, like the head of a person. It lives in the big box under the
screen, it gets warm when it works, and we never open the box or push anything inside it.

**Let us talk**

1. Which part of you does your thinking?
2. What happens when you press a key on the keyboard?
3. Why must we keep our fingers out of the big box?
4. Name two other machines that have a thinking part.


![A boy stands beside a big plain box on a table, one finger raised, with a small square chip resting on top of the box and air lines at its side.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-digital-literacy/week2-the-thinking-box.png)
**Worksheet**

1. The part of the computer that does the thinking is the ____ . A) shoe B) CPU C) bag D) chair
2. The CPU is like the ____ of a person. A) elbow B) toe C) finger D) head
3. The CPU lives inside the ____ . A) lunch box B) school bag C) big box under the screen D) shoe rack
4. The box has small holes so that the CPU can get ____ . A) air B) water C) sunlight D) sleep
5. Fill in: We must not ____ the big box of the computer.
6. Mention three machines that have a thinking part.
7. Write two sentences on why the CPU gets warm.

**My own work**

Draw a big box with a small square inside it and dots for the air holes, then draw a line from the box to a screen.

---

### WEEK 3 — RAM: THE MEMORY THAT HOLDS THINGS A LITTLE WHILE

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Digital Literacy · Third Term W3.*

**You will learn to**

• say what RAM is for
• tell how RAM is different from the hard drive
• say what happens to RAM when power goes off
• name one thing we do before we lose our work

**Things to know**

• **RAM** is the computer's **short memory**; it holds what we are working on **just now**.
• RAM is like **carrying things in both hands**: it is fast, but the hands can hold only so much.
• When the machine is **switched off**, what was in RAM is **gone** — the hands become empty.
• The **hard drive** is different: it keeps things **until tomorrow**, like a box in the cupboard.
• If we write a name on the computer and never **save** it, the power failure will take it away.
• More RAM means the computer can hold **more work at the same time**, like two hands and not one.
• RAM lives **inside the big box**, flat on the same board as the CPU, in a long thin strip.
• A **power failure** is a common way to lose work, so we **save as we go**, not only at the end.
• Little things in RAM are **fast**; big things kept in the hard drive are **slower to bring out**.
• A machine with too little RAM **pauses**; a patient child waits and does not hit the keyboard.

**Words for my notebook**

RAM is the computer's short memory, like work held in two hands; it is emptied when power goes off. The
hard drive keeps things until tomorrow, so we save as we go and do not wait for the end.

**Let us talk**

1. What holds your work when the machine is still on?
2. What happens to that work when power goes off?
3. Where does the machine keep a thing until tomorrow?
4. Why do we save before we finish?


![A girl carries three blocks in both hands while four more blocks sit inside an open box with its lid leaning against it.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-digital-literacy/week3-held-in-two-hands.png)
**Worksheet**

1. The computer's short memory is called ____ . A) the bag B) the box C) the screen D) RAM
2. RAM holds the work we are doing ____ . A) just now B) last year C) next week D) yesterday
3. When the machine is switched off, RAM becomes ____ . A) heavier B) bigger C) empty D) warm
4. The part that keeps our work until tomorrow is the ____ . A) cup B) pencil C) keyboard D) hard drive
5. Fill in: We save our work because a ____ may take it away.
6. Mention two things that make a computer pause or work slowly.
7. Write two sentences on what you do so that your work is not lost.

**My own work**

Draw two hands carrying three blocks each, then draw an empty box beside them with the blocks inside it.

---

### WEEK 4 — THE HARD DRIVE: THE BOX THAT KEEPS OUR WORK

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Digital Literacy · Third Term W4.*

**You will learn to**

• say what the hard drive keeps
• tell where our picture and our song are kept
• name one thing that hurts a hard drive
• say the word for putting work back into the machine

**Things to know**

• The **hard drive** is the computer's **keeping box**; what goes in there stays until we take it out.
• Our **drawing**, our **song** and the **story we wrote** live in the hard drive.
• **Save** means to put work into the keeping box; **open** means to bring it out again.
• A hard drive can hold **very many** things — a thousand pictures and more — yet it is small.
• Some keeping boxes live **outside** the machine on a **small stick** called a flash drive.
• A **flash drive** is small enough for a pocket and can be carried to another machine.
• A hard drive does not like a **knock** or a **shake** while it is working, so a running machine stays still.
• If the keeping box is **full**, we throw away old songs and pictures the way we clear a shelf.
• The hard drive sits **inside the big box** on the same board as the CPU and RAM.
• We do not pull the plug out of a machine that is still saving, or the work may be lost.

**Words for my notebook**

The hard drive is the keeping box of the computer: our picture, our song and our story live there until we
open them again. A flash drive is a small keeping box that can go into a pocket and to another machine.

**Let us talk**

1. Where does the computer keep your drawing until tomorrow?
2. What do we call putting your work into the keeping box?
3. What is a flash drive like in your house?
4. Why must a machine that is working stay still?


![A boy kneels by an open box and puts a card inside, with a house picture, a disc and a folded paper stored there, and a small drive beside it.](assets/img/nursery-2/3rd-term/basic-digital-literacy/week4-the-keeping-box.png)
**Worksheet**

1. The keeping box of the computer is the ____ . A) hard drive B) school cap C) door D) spoon
2. To put work into the keeping box we say ____ . A) open B) break C) save D) sing
3. A small keeping box we can carry in a pocket is a ____ . A) plate B) cup C) pillow D) flash drive
4. A hard drive does not like ____ while it is working. A) sleep B) a knock or a shake C) a fan D) a cover
5. Fill in: If the keeping box is full, we ____ away old pictures.
6. Mention three things we may keep in a hard drive.
7. Write two sentences on the last thing you saved on a machine.

**My own work**

Draw a box with a picture, a note and a song card inside it, then draw a small stick with its own lid beside it.

---

### WEEK 5 — THE MOTHERBOARD: THE FLOOR EVERY PART SITS ON

*Depth: NERDC Nursery 3 · Basic Digital Literacy · Third Term W5.*

**You will learn to**

• say what a motherboard is
• name three parts that sit on it
• tell how parts pass news to one another
• say why water is the enemy of a board

**Things to know**

• The **motherboard** is the **big flat board** inside the box that **joins all the parts together**.
• It is like the **floor of a house**: the CPU, the RAM and the hard drive all sit on it and are connected.
• Thin **lines** run over the board like **roads**, and news travels along them from one part to another.
• A **port** is a little door on the board where a wire from the keyboard or the mouse is put in.
• The **cable** is the wire that carries news and power from one thing to another.
• If one part is loose, the news cannot pass, and the machine **refuses to work** — like a road with a hole.
• **Water** is the enemy of a board: it leaves spots that break the little roads.
• A **spilled cup** on a laptop can finish the whole machine, so drink sits on the other table.
• We never touch the board of an opened machine; the power inside can **give a child a shock**.
• Small **metal pins** hold parts to the board; they are for a grown-up's tool, not for a finger.

**Words for my notebook**

The motherboard is the big flat board inside the computer's box. The CPU, the RAM and the hard drive sit on
it, and news passes between them along thin lines like roads, which is why water must never touch a board.

**Let us talk**

1. What is the board inside the box called?
2. Which three parts sit on that board?
