# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — NIGERIAN HISTORY (Nursery 2 · 3rd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 53 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. A traditional ruler is the ___ of the people in his area
   A) head
   B) trader
   C) visitor
   D) child
2. A traditional ruler is chosen into the throne by ___
   A) the monitor of the class
   B) the elders of the ruling house
   C) the children of the school
   D) the women of the market
3. When two families are fighting they are taken to the ruler to be ___
   A) laughed at
   B) forgotten
   C) divided
   D) settled
4. The ruler keeps our ___ safe for the people
   A) the cups in the market
   B) the fees of the school
   C) customs and festivals
   D) the shoes of the house
5. An Emir is the traditional ruler of the ___
   A) North
   B) South
   C) river bank
   D) coast
6. The Oba is known by his long ___
   A) school bag
   B) beads of coral
   C) fishing net
   D) broom
7. In the Southeast the ruler is called an Igwe or an ___
   A) pilot
   B) Oba
   C) Emir
   D) Eze
8. Many thrones in one country teach the people to ___
   A) honour one another
   B) sell the land
   C) forget one another
   D) divide the market
9. A chief is a ___ who speaks for a group of families
   A) stranger
   B) title holder
   C) child
   D) trader
10. Titles are mostly given to people who ___
   A) come from a very far place
   B) shout the loudest in the market
   C) tell the truth and help the community
   D) own many goats in the village
11. A title is given by the people and then ___ by the ruler
   A) denied
   B) forgotten
   C) sold
   D) confirmed
12. A title is a honour that ___
   A) is a toy
   B) cannot be bought
   C) can be bought
   D) lasts one day
13. The father of the village street is called the ___
   A) Stranger
   B) Iyaloja
   C) Baale
   D) Otunba
14. The mother of the market is called the ___
   A) Iyaloja
   B) Baale
   C) Baby
   D) Otunba
15. The chief who carries messages to the quarters is the ___
   A) child
   B) Otunba
   C) farmer
   D) visitor
16. Title holders are respected as ___, not as rich persons
   A) traders
   B) friends
   C) institutions
   D) singers
17. The nearest government to the people is the ___
   A) Local Government
   B) the church on Sunday
   C) the market on Friday
   D) the palace at home
18. The head of a Local Government is called the ___
   A) king
   B) Babalawo
   C) class teacher
   D) Executive Chairman
19. A chairman comes into office by ___
   A) buying the chair
   B) voting
   C) a race
   D) a coin toss
20. To vote for a chairman one must be ___ years or more
   A) two
   B) ten
   C) eighteen
   D) four
21. A chairman sits in his office for ___
   A) one hour
   B) one day
   C) the whole of his life
   D) a fixed time
22. A chairman clears the ___ before the rain comes
   A) drains and gutters
   B) the books in the class
   C) the dogs at the gate
   D) the birds in the tree
23. A leader who does not take what is not his is called ___
   A) sleepy
   B) tall
   C) honest
   D) rich
24. A good leader ___ before he decides
   A) runs away
   B) hits the table
   C) closes his ears
   D) listens to both sides
25. A child chosen to keep order in the class is the ___
   A) baby
   B) class monitor
   C) visitor
   D) trader
26. A prefect is a leader for ___
   A) the whole school
   B) the market
   C) one house only
   D) his friends only
27. Before I ask the class to be quiet I must be ___ myself
   A) loud
   B) singing
   C) running
   D) quiet
28. A ruler and a chairman together give the town ___
   A) new shoes
   B) only rain
   C) wisdom and money
   D) a holiday
29. A person who goes in front and keeps order is a ___
   A) leader
   B) trader
   C) stranger
   D) sleeper
30. If a chairman does not do his work the people ___
   A) will give him a new horse
   B) will not vote for him again
   C) will send a chief to him
   D) will build him a fine house

## Section B — Sub-objective (10 marks)
Answer briefly.

32. Mention three rulers of Nigeria and where each rules
33. Name two things a chief may wear to show his title
34. Say who the Baale is and who the Iyaloja is
35. Name two services a Local Government provides
36. Say how a chairman is chosen and how a ruler is chosen
37. Mention two things that make a leader good
38. Name two jobs a class monitor does
39. Say two places a chairman's money should be seen
40. Mention two differences between a ruler and a chairman

## Section C — Theory (10 marks)
Answer in full sentences.

   C) bush

23. A village sends ___ to the town

   B) baskets of food

   C) only on Sunday
   D) together

   B) runs away from
   C) guides and serves
   D) looks down on

   B) pupil
   C) seller
   D) driver
27. One quality of a good leader is being a good ___
   A) eater
   B) listener
   C) sleeper
   D) runner
28. Nigeria has ___ tongues and cultures
   A) two
   B) no
   C) one
   D) many
29. Amala and efo, garri and pounded yam are ___ of our people
   A) foods
   B) tools
   C) games
   D) songs
30. A child in another country ___
   A) has no tongue at all
   B) eats nothing at all
   C) goes to school and plays
   D) stays from school

Answer briefly.

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — YORUBA (Nursery 2 · 3rd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 54 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. "Ẹ jó!" tells the class to ____
   A) sit down
   B) begin reading
   C) stand up
   D) wash the hand
2. To make a command polite we put in the word ____
   A) ṣé
   B) jọ̀wọ́
   C) àáṣẹ̀
   D) ṣeé
3. "Ẹ káàlẹ́" is the greeting said in ____
   A) the morning
   B) the class
   C) the evening
   D) the market
4. When an elder is greeted, a girl ____ and a boy lies full length.
   A) kneels
   B) jumps
   C) runs
   D) shouts
5. "à-kà-rá" said together gives the word ____
   A) kàrà
   B) àrá
   C) àkà
   D) àkàrà
6. How many sounds is the word "bàtà" made of? ____
   A) one
   B) two
   C) four
   D) six
7. The fruit of the palm that is pressed for red oil is ____
   A) ọ̀pẹ̀
   B) áńbà
   C) àmùtì
   D) bílíbilí
8. An àlọ́ àpamọ́ is the kind of story that ____
   A) ends in a lie
   B) counts the days
   C) is only a song
   D) hides a lesson
9. The call "Àlọ́ o!" takes the reply ____
   A) À ń ṣé!
   B) Àpamọ́!
   C) Ẹ ṣé gan!
   D) Báwo ni o!
10. When a child wants to reply to the teacher he says ____
   A) Ó dára
   B) Moò ṣé
   C) Mi ò
   D) Lọ kúrò
11. If a word is too fast for us we say "Ẹ jọ̀wọ́, ____"
   A) lọ kúrò
   B) wò ó
   C) fi sí i
   D) tún un sọ
12. The body is washed at òwúrọ̀ and again ____
   A) in the evening
   B) on Monday
   C) twice a year
   D) in a month
13. Hands wash with soap before food and after ____
   A) the song
   B) the toilet
   C) the drum
   D) the market gate
14. Twenty in Yoruba is ____
   A) àrún
   B) ogótà
   C) ogún
   D) ẹ́wàá
15. "Ogún àti ẹ̀ta" is the number ____
   A) 32
   B) 40
   C) 13
   D) 23
16. Forty is said in Yoruba as ____
   A) ogótà
   B) ogún mẹ́rin
   C) ọ̀rúnmọ́lẹ́dógún
   D) ọ̀ọ́dúnrún
17. Ogún àti ọ̀kan plus ọ̀kan is ____
   A) ogún àti ọ̀kan
   B) ogún nìkan
   C) ogún àti èjì
   D) ogún àti ẹ̀ta
18. The day of thanks and rest is called ____
   A) Ọjọ́rú
   B) Ọjọ́ Àìkú
   C) Ọjọ́ Ayé
   D) Ọjọ́bọ̀
19. On Ọjọ́ Àbámẹ́ta the children ____
   A) sleep all day
   B) work on the farm
   C) count the pots
   D) sweep and play
20. "Àwọ̀ ewé" is the colour of ____
   A) a stone
   B) new milk
   C) a leaf
   D) the sky
21. The Yoruba for red is ____
   A) púpà
   B) funfun
   C) àwọ̀ ilẹ̀
   D) dúdú
22. "Iṣẹ́ ni òògùn ìẹ́" says the medicine for shame is ____
   A) play
   B) work
   C) sleep
   D) wealth
23. The elder sibling, who leads by the hand, is the ____
   A) ọmọ ìyá
   B) àbúrò
   C) ẹ̀gbọ́n
   D) àgbàlagbà
24. Before she takes a thing, a younger child always ____
   A) runs off
   B) hides it
   C) laughs loud
   D) asks first
25. "Kí ni n ń fi ọ̀lẹ̀ ṣe?" is the question inside a ____
   A) market call

   D) lullaby
26. Before the pot goes on the fire the wet flour is pressed so that the ____ goes out.

   B) oil
   C) sand
   D) smoke
27. "Fa kọ́kọ́ yọ" in the akọ̀sọ̀rọ̀ drill means to ____
   A) sweep the yard
   B) fetch the water
   C) eat the food
   D) pull out the knot
28. "Ahọ́n" is the ____
   A) neck
   B) nose
   C) tongue
   D) ear
29. The beaded crown worn by the Òba is his ____
   A) àdé
   B) ẹ̀kẹ́
   C) ìka
   D) asọ̀
30. Over a village, under the Òba, is the ____
   A) Aláàfin
   B) Baale
   C) Ọọni
   D) Olúbadan

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — MATHEMATICS & ENGLISH · LITERACY (LANGUAGE DOMAIN) (Nursery 2 · 3rd Term)

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)

   A) blend
   B) sight word
   C) trigraph
   D) digraph

   A) no sound at all
   B) two sounds apart
   C) a long line
   D) one sound

   A) cat
   B) atc
   C) tch
   D) ach

   A) dress
   B) chair
   C) shirt
   D) clock

   A) digraph
   B) naming word
   C) plural mark
   D) blend
6. A noun is a word that ____
   A) shows a size
   B) ends a line
   C) tells the time
   D) names a thing

   A) school
   B) Bola
   C) river
   D) chair
8. "One child, two ____"
   A) children
   B) childs
   C) childes
   D) childrens
9. A big letter is put on ____
   A) a full stop
   B) a colour word
   C) a name like Lagos
   D) a doing word

   A) big and tall
   B) run and jump
   C) there and cat
   D) old and young

   A) owns
   B) does
   C) is called
   D) looks like

   A) sing
   B) singed
   C) sings
   D) singing

   B) Where
   C) Who
   D) When

   A) table
   B) green
   C) small
   D) clap

   A) colour
   B) doing
   C) long
   D) small
16. Which of these is NOT yet a sentence?
   A) The happy child.
   B) Fish swim.
   C) Dogs run.
   D) The girl jumps.

   A) teaches
   B) drives
   C) flies
   D) swims

   A) Then
   B) They
   C) Your
   D) There

   A) no other word
   B) another naming word
   C) a plural mark
   D) a big letter only
20. The word that brings a reason is ____
   A) because
   B) there
   C) them
   D) and
21. "I see the boys" can be said as "I see ____"
   A) they're
   B) there
   C) them
   D) then

   B) Why do I run?

   D) last

   C) Where?
   D) Why?

   C) erase
   D) guess
26. Which word opens the old tale line "____ upon a time"?

   C) Only
   D) Over
27. "The ball is ____" — the word that tells a place is ____
   A) they
   B) their
   C) there
   D) then

   A) us
   B) them
   C) there
   D) their

   A) slowly with your finger

   B) big
   C) round
   D) small

   B) full stop

   D) the

   C) small
   D) quiet

   B) five

   D) six

   C) sh
   D) ee

   B) pen
   C) cup

   B) sounds

   D) lines

   B) sight
   C) long
   D) small

   C) sunset
   D) rainbow

   A) play in

   C) character

   C) end
   D) age

   B) like
   C) named
   D) worth

   C) the
   D) one
19. Which of these is the opposite of "big"?
   A) thin
   B) tall
   C) long
   D) small

   A) fast
   B) slow
   C) happy
   D) hot
21. An action word tells us what somebody ____
   A) has
   B) owns
   C) does
   D) is called

   A) swim

   C) girl
   D) the

   B) runned
   C) running

   D) burn

   D) exclamation

   B) small
   C) round
   D) quiet

   D) mark

# EXAMINATION PRACTICE — MATHEMATICS & ENGLISH · NUMERACY (Nursery 2 · 3rd Term)

**Time:** 1 hour · **Total: 54 marks**

## Section A — Objective (30 marks)
Choose the correct option and ring the letter.

1. When a cake is shared so that both parts match, each part is a ____
   A) half
   B) whole
   C) quarter
   D) side

   A) a quarter
   B) nothing
   C) the whole
   D) one half

   A) halves
   B) quarters
   C) edges
   D) corners

   A) three
   B) four
   C) six
   D) five

   A) wide
   B) heavy
   C) deep
   D) long

   A) paper
   B) water
   C) body
   D) tin
7. One metre is the same as ____ centimetres

   B) 100
   C) 1000
   D) 10
8. Before we measure with a ruler we begin at the line marked ____
   A) zero
   B) thirty
   C) seven
   D) twelve

   A) breaks off
   B) stays up
   C) goes down
   D) turns round
10. Weight of a thing tells us how ____ it is

   B) hot
   C) loud
   D) heavy

   A) neither one
   B) the balloon
   C) both alike
   D) the stone

   A) centimetre
   B) hand span
   C) kilogram
   D) litre

   A) evening
   B) midday
   C) tomorrow
   D) yesterday
14. How many days make one week? ____
   A) seven
   B) five
   C) twelve
   D) ten
15. At 9 o'clock the long hand of the clock points at ____
   A) 9
   B) 12
   C) 3
   D) 6
16. The short hand of a clock counts the ____
   A) months
   B) minutes
   C) hours
   D) days
17. A ball is shaped like a ____
   A) sphere
   B) cube
   C) pyramid
   D) cone
18. How many flat faces has a cube? ____
   A) eight
   B) four
   C) ten
   D) six
19. A tin of milk is shaped like a ____
   A) cone
   B) cylinder
   C) square
   D) sphere
20. The place where two faces of a box meet is its ____
   A) edge
   B) shadow
   C) middle
   D) corner
21. A triangle has ____ sides.
   A) six
   B) four
   C) five
   D) three
22. Which of these shapes has no corner at all? ____
   A) a square
   B) a triangle
   C) a circle
   D) a rectangle

   A) cone
   B) rectangle
   C) circle
   D) sphere

   A) a cone
   B) an oval
   C) a circle
   D) a square

   A) cube
   B) circle
   C) square
   D) triangle

   A) symmetry
   B) counting
   C) weight
   D) length

   A) triangle
   B) square
   C) oval
   D) circle

   A) four

   C) three
   D) five

   B) the same
   C) fewer
   D) no

   A) draw a big sun

   D) dust

   A) a stone
   B) a feather
   C) a dry leaf
   D) a cork
9. We waste water when we leave the ____ running
   A) fan
   B) lamp
   C) tap
   D) door
10. The part of the day when the sun is highest and hottest is ____
   A) morning
   B) evening
   C) night
   D) midday
11. At night we see the moon and the ____ in the sky
   A) kites
   B) stars
   C) boats
   D) planes
12. Rain comes down out of the ____
   A) ground
   B) market
   C) sky
   D) well
13. A wheel makes a heavy load easy to move because it ____
   A) rolls
   B) floats
   C) rusts
   D) melts
14. A plank laid from the ground up to a lorry is a ____
   A) keg
   B) bucket
   C) lever
   D) ramp
15. To lift a big stone with a strong stick is to use a ____
   A) rake
   B) lever
   C) broom
   D) ruler
16. A pair of scissors is passed to a friend by the ____
   A) spring
   B) blade
   C) handle
   D) point
17. Nigeria took its name from the ____
   A) river Niger
   B) river Benue
   C) long mountain
   D) big lake
18. The colours of our flag, read in order, are ____
   A) red, green, red
   B) green, red, white
   C) white, green, white
   D) green, white, green
19. The Niger and the Benue, our two big rivers, meet ____
   A) at the top of the map
   B) in the middle
   C) only in the north
   D) far at the sea
20. A child who is different from me is treated ____
   A) the same
   B) last
   C) never
   D) first
21. Twenty bottle tops put in two equal lines make lines of ____
   A) five
   B) fifteen
   C) eight
   D) ten
22. The day that comes right after Friday is ____
   A) Sunday
   B) Wednesday
   C) Saturday
   D) Monday
23. A book is like which of these shapes? ____
   A) a ball
   B) a square
   C) a circle
   D) a cone
24. Which of these is a vegetable and not a fruit? ____
   A) ugu
   B) mango
   C) orange
   D) banana
25. The worker whose labour makes sure the whole town eats is the ____
   A) barber
   B) guard
   C) driver
   D) farmer
26. In the stories of our people, the small one that out-thinks the whole bush is ____
   A) the horse
   B) the elephant
   C) the tortoise
   D) the lion
27. Queen Amina of Zaria is remembered in story as ____
   A) a boat man
   B) a warrior queen
   C) a drum maker
   D) a market seller
28. A hero is a person who ____
   A) takes the big share
   B) runs from trouble
   C) hides his mistake
   D) finishes the work begun
29. In the game "Where does it live?", a hen lives ____
   A) in the forest
   B) in the river
   C) in the house yard
   D) in the sky
30. "I come out in the day and give the world light and warmth. Who am I?" — ____
   A) the sun
   B) the star
   C) the lamp
   D) the moon

Answer in full sentences.
